## Sunday, June 24: 8:48 p.m. - Quarterfinals: Guillaume Wafo-Tapa vs. Shouta Yasooka

*by Kyle Sanchez*

 

![Wild Pair](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Wild+Pair)

This match had the newly crowned Resident Genius, Guillaume Wafo-Tapa, opposite last years Player of the Year, Shouta Yasooka.

Wafo-Tapa was playing an insanely brilliant [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) combo deck featuring [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion) and a host of naughty slivers including Dormant, Gemhide, Telekinetic, Frenetic; and Reflex to give them all haste. He used a lot of mana accelerants like [Coalition Relic](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Coalition+Relic) and [Prismatic Lens](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prismatic+Lens) to speed up to [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) mana and [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion) to chain through his entire deck putting literally all of his slivers in play.

Shouta on the other hand was playing a standard UB list featuring the tried and true [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings) engine along with Teferi and friends. How. Droll.

I haven't actually seen Mr. Wafo-Tapa in action so I'm not too sure what to expect. The deck looks like a mediocre draft deck without any of the powerhouse slivers. The real advantage to playing such a bizzare deck is that no one knows how to fight against it.

### Game 1

Wafo-Tapa started out with a [Gemhide Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemhide+Sliver) which powered out a [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee) setting up his next few turns. Yasooka began with a pair of [Terramorphic Expanse](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terramorphic+Expanse) along with some charge action on his [Dreadship Reef](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreadship+Reef). When Wafo-Tapa played a [Frenetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frenetic+Sliver) and [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) on his next turn Yasooka took the opportunity to [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) the pesky slivers away. Frenetic stayed around thanks to an obviously rigged die and a [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver) came down for Guillaume.

Wafo-Tapa tried a [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) followed up by a Gemhide next turn, when Yasooka responded with a [Slaughter Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Pact) on [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver). Wafo-Tapa saved it via the die roll with [Frenetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frenetic+Sliver)'s ability which also replenished his hand. Another [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) from Yasooka saw Guillaume saving all three of his slivers, and filling Wafo-Tapa hand up with even more cards.

![Frenetic Sliver](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Frenetic+Sliver)

Guillaume tapped out to play [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) and things were looking bad for the POY. Shouta stole [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) via a [Take Possession](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Take+Possession) on the following turn, but Wafo-Tapa still had a hand full of slivery goodies and the luck of the dice on his side. Wafo-Tapa took the opportunity to land a [Telekinetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Telekinetic+Sliver) which started to form a chokehold on Yasooka's mana.

The next few turns saw Wafo-Tapa tripling the number of Slivers under his control and a final [Frenetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frenetic+Sliver) activation on [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver) enabled him to attack in for victory with the sliver nation.

Wafo-Tapa: 1  
 Yasooka: 0

Game 2:

The first few turns saw both players advancing their mana, Yaso with [Dreadship Reef](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreadship+Reef), and Wafo-Tapa with [Coalition Relic](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Coalition+Relic). The game was moving at a snails pace at this point with both players literally doing nothing but playing draw-go. I'm sure it would go faster if I didn't have to wee so bad.

Guillaume finally initiated some "action" by taking Yasooka's [Dreadship Reef](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreadship+Reef) with a [Take Possession](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Take+Possession).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/12/ed/12ed0f91158704adf2ca172e54af72ed09792affbdc7ff9a3b40390328eb4493.jpg)*Shouta couldn't contain the legion*

...

...

...

Yup, still nothing happening. I swear I could have gone to a restroom in Japan, came back, and they would still be on Wafo-Tapa's turn with no relevant permanents in play.

Flash forward a million turns and two [Gemhide Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemhide+Sliver)s along with a [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) are in play for Guillaume and Yasooka ain't lookin' too hot, however he did manage to get another exciting [Prismatic Lens](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prismatic+Lens) into play before shipping it back to Guillaume.

Guillaume had landed a [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver) and started a [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion) loop, which Yasooka tried to [Strangling Soot](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Strangling+Soot). Unfortunately Wafo-Tapa had the [Mystic Snake](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Snake) and Whitemane finally returned to Guillaume's hand after fetching a [Reflex Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflex+Sliver). Wafo-Tapa has the game nearly wrapped up at this point since he could use instant speed [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion)'s to fetch [Mystic Snake](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Snake) to stop whatever disruption Yasooka could have.

Yasooka tried a pair of [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation), one countered, but the other one sticked as it appeared Guillaume was out of [Mystic Snake](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Snake)'s. Instead he saved 4 of his 7 slivers and used Whitemane to fetch another Frenetic.

DAMNATION RESOLVED!!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/12/ad/12ade762c4e9094480a6f715ae92ec2aa44d29441dba7ecd1f03bf63ade3297e.jpg)*Wafo-Tapa ponders how he wants to win.*

When all the slivers came back into play Guillame drew 9 cards, resolved a [Telekinetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Telekinetic+Sliver), and suspended a [Detritivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detritivore) for three before passing the turn.

During Yasooka's upkeep Wafo-Tapa tapped him down before Yasooka flashed back [Strangling Soot](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Strangling+Soot) on [Telekinetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Telekinetic+Sliver), which was saved via Mr. Frenetic. Also during Yasooka's draw phase Guillaume used [Extirpate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extirpate) to strip Yasooka of all of his [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation)s.

To finish things off Teneb showed up for Wafo-Tapa getting back a [Reflex Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflex+Sliver), which drew two cards via Dormant, which tapped down lands during Shouta's upkeep, which forced Shouta to offer his hand to the resident genius.

Guillaume Wafo-Tapa 2-0 over Shouta Yasooka.

  

## Sunday, June 24: 9:00 p.m. - Quarterfinals: Celso Zampere vs. Koutarou Ootsuka

*by Noah Weil*

 

### Game 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/66/26/66266558e41186cec71c8698080ddcc9aea692a24c8a6fb3d0fdf666cf0612c5.jpg)*A tough spot.*

Celso started off poorly in his first game of the top 8 with a *triple* mulligan. Yet for four cards it wasn't too bad. All of his colors were available and a [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) was cast to bring the beats. Ootsuka's draw was fine, including an early [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots). Celso tried to break out with elephants, but a Venser from the Japanese player took out one of the 3/3s, granting Ootsuka even more time to get his deck online. Triple [Horizon Canopy](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horizon+Canopy) gave Zampere some refuel options, but it also took away plenty of life, a wedge Ootsuka was happy to squeeze through. A remaining elephant held off two morphs and Venser, but with only lands and [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide) to work with, Celso's clock was winding down. Another [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) allowed a token to actually attack, but the defending 3/3 vanished into the aether off [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate). [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) from Celso, allowed and forced him to shuffle in an RFGed [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) into his library, mattering not at all as the remaining token was bounced by another Cloudskate. When Ootsuka flashed the third Riftwing, Zampere packed it in.

Zampere-0  
 Ootsuka-1

Celso shuffled furiously after this game, but what had that done for him for the first?

### Game 2

It worked out this time; both players kept their original seven. Celso came out first with a graft land and Saffi, but Koutarou had the mirror champion in [Penumbra Spider](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Penumbra+Spider). Barring [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation), that Spider was a real problem for a G/W deck to get through, never mind twice. Celso decided to hedge his bets against bounce, with a 3/3 Avenger and 3/3 grafted [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper), rather than a potential breakthrough 4/4 Serra. His caution was rewarded when Venser bounced the angel and ganged up with the Spider to block the [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper). A [Thrill of the Hunt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrill+of+the+Hunt) saved the enhanced [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper), and took down the first incarnation of the 2/4.

![Serra Avenger](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Serra+Avenger)

Celso considered and attacked with an Avenger and a 3/3, with Thrill in the grave. Avenger + Thrill took down the blocking spider token, while the Armodon-esque struck Koutarou. Another Avenger hit play for Celso and now it was multiple Angels against a spiderless opponent. Not even a [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental) lock would stop the vigilant 3/3s. Both Avengers came in and dealt a punishing 6. Unafraid of the G/U wrath, [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) was played post-combat to further shore up any remaining defenses. At the end of the turn, one morph became a [Thelonite Hermit](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thelonite+Hermit), packing buddies. Were the second morph another Hermit or Shapeshifter, there would be a lot of very large tokens suddenly in play, but no such luck. A [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate) bought the Japanese player some invaluable time, another turn to hunt for a Shapeshifter. Celso looked visibly annoyed at the bounce creature, yet he pulled it together and studied the board. In came the Avenger, unblocked and bringing Ootsuka to a mere 6 life. Another Riftwing bounced that same Avenger and again the original attacked. A double block from Ootsuka ended badly, as one [Thrill of the Hunt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrill+of+the+Hunt) killed two. Ootsuka couldn't come back from that and conceded.

Ootsuka-1  
 Zampere-1

### Game 3

![](https://i.ormos.uk/bf/f1/bff1093f3ca7639ac8915d9370683376474cca0354746a3f245619279348ec08.jpg)*Celso Zampere studies the board.*

Both kept for this final game. Celso led with my favorite move of the tournament, Flagstones and a cycled [Edge of Autumn](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Edge+of+Autumn). Ootsuka wasn't doing badly, with [Search for Tomorrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Search+for+Tomorrow) and a [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots). Would it be enough to stop [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)? It's only 0/5…

[Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate) bounced one 'Goyf, which in turn took a [Sunlance](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunlance). [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee) from Ootsuka gave him options, especially with the top four being undesirable, but both players knew [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) was the largest creature on the table and Celso was in the driver's seat. A morph from Ootsuka threatened a [Thelonite Hermit](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thelonite+Hermit) overwhelming, but another [Sunlance](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunlance) took it down. A second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) ended in a vicious turn for the Brazilian. Celso's next turn was even stronger, a *third* 'Goyf and a [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger) in the air. Untapped Clouskate, 0/1 [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots), Penumbra spider and a facedown from the Japanese gave Zempere pause, but with a [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation) in hand, Zampere went for it. In came every creature, and now the onus was on Ootsuka. Both Spider and Riftwing blocked the Avenger, letting all three Goyfs through for 9 points. After damage was stacked Venser rescued an Isolated creature, killing [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger) but putting a new card type in the 'yard. Triple 4/5s were tough, especially at five life to an opponent's 13. Down came the spider again, and in came the creatures. One [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) came through, bringing Koutarou to a scant one life. Celso's [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger) even removed [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental) from the equation. Koutarou had to concede, to the consolation of his countrymen. Zampere gave a whoop, to the cheers of his.

Ootsuka-1  
 Zampere-2

  

## Sunday, June 24: 9:03 p.m. - Quarterfinals: Kenji Tsumura vs. Gabriel Schwartz

*by Eric Reasoner*

 

### Game 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c9/58/c958c91dbe7577d0d4adacbd1c93032a8f94edae8add5df1299af6ead27ed2fe.jpg)*Top 8 competitor Gabe Schwartz.*

Game 1 took all of 3 minutes. Kenji suspended [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision)s, [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate), [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision)s, Riftwing Clouskate. Gabriel meanwhile was putting [Kavu Predator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kavu+Predator)s to work. On turn four he pointed a [Fiery Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiery+Justice) at Kenji's face to make each of his Kavus 7/7. Swing for 14? All Kenji could do was untap, draw his card and get ready for the next game.

Gabriel- "Hey, it's what the deck does"

Tsumura- 0  
 Schwartz- 1

Kenji pulled [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision)s and [Serrated Arrows](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serrated+Arrows) for [Snapback](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapback)s, Vensers and [Take Possession](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Take+Possession)s.

### Game 2

![Willbender](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Willbender)

Kenji lead with a suspended Cloudskate. Another Predator for Gabriel, this time off of a [Grove of the Burnwillows](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grove+of+the+Burnwillows); clearly the Kavu's homeland. The players spent the next few turn making [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s and morphs and coy, come-hither looks to each other. [Fiery Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiery+Justice) from Gabriel cleared most of Kenji's board and allowed him to get in for a fight. What began as a solid start for Gabriel quickly turned sour when Kenji made a face-up [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental) and followed that up with a [Vesuvan Shapeshifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vesuvan+Shapeshifter). A combo that has served the "best player in the world" well all weekend. Praying for an untap step, Gabriel attempted a Dead on the Shapeshifter during Kenji's before he could flip it down. Kenji responded with a [Delay](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delay) for the Dead to keep Gabriel's untap step a remote dream. Neither player could fight, but it was clear that Gabriel was in bad shape and would be hard-pressed to make a game of it. A [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate) finally gave Kenji a clock. Gabriel wondered allowed if there was "anything I can do." He finally decided 'no' and shuffled up for game 3.

Gabriel-"Who knew that needing to untap would be that important?"

Tsumura- 1  
 Schwartz- 1

### Game 3

Gabe started game 3 running, putting Kenji to 11 with a Saffi and a [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) token. After some trading and killing the board was empty. With nothing on the field, Gabriel put a [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) into play. Kenji paused for a long while trying to decide what to do. **Magic** must be a terrible chore for the Kenji; when he goes into the think tank he looks like he's in physical pain. His body slightly writhing, his hands always on his face. He makes **Magic** look excruciating.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/38/b2/38b292eb4621809508dc37da333d1b7c74520d5b765a3273c4f94bfe9df5393b.jpg)*Kavu Predator in the motherland.*

Finally the littlest champion went with a [Snapback](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapback) for the giant threat. Mystic again goes back home, this time due to a Venser. Kenji now had two morphs and a Venser caught in [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation) to Gabriel's clear board. Gabe put a [Kavu Predator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kavu+Predator) into play and aimed a [Fiery Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiery+Justice) at the two morphs. Unfortunately there was very little the face-down [Willbender](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Willbender) could do about it.

Kenji was now in a tenuous position, facing down a 7/7 [Kavu Predator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kavu+Predator) with only a [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental) in hand. He took a hit from the Kavu to go to 7 and watched the Mystic hit the board. He played Briny, flipped it up and said go. Gabe decided to hold back on hitting with his Mystic and passed the turn. Kenji then played another morph, and Gabriel gave the 'That can't be [Vesuva](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vesuva)n can it?' smile. It was, and Gabe knew he wouldn't be untapping for some time to come. But Kenji wasn't out of the woods yet, sitting on 7 life and staring down a 6/6 flyer, things could still go haywire. Trying to swarm around Kenji's defenses, Gabriel made a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) which fell to [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel). He groaned, wondering how this game went so wrong, so fast. Kenji then played another morph and attacked with the face-down Shapeshifter, putting Gabriel into an awkward position. Pointing to the other morph, Gabriel asked, "That's a Shapeshifter right? Show it to me." It was and Kenji did and Gabriel extended the hand.

Tsumura- 2  
 Schwartz- 1

  

## Sunday, June 24: 9:08 p.m. - Quarterfinals: Paul Cheon vs. Jason Imperiale

*by Craig Jones*

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/3f/d7/3fd72c44f98c24650ecf8b29d80feef088b125502152a09c25ff44c11e1ae9f7.jpg)*Jason Imperiale*

It's Sunday afternoon and we're now down to just eight competitors remaining. Paul Cheon is the current US national champion and while he was relatively unknown to the rest of the world at the time he's since built on that success with a solid string of finishes. He's running the updated version of the blue-black control deck with additions such as [Korlash, Heir to Blackblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Korlash%2C+Heir+to+Blackblade) and [River of Tears](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=River+of+Tears) from Future Sight.

Jason Imperiale is running the white-green [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) deck that's made a big impact this weekend.

Cheon won the die roll and hit Imperiale with a [Psychotic Episode](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychotic+Episode). Imperiale had to read the card to remind himself what it did. Cheon followed up with a [Careful Consideration](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Consideration) and thought for a bit before discarding two land.

Imperiale just did what beatdown does. He played out [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and [Mire Boa](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mire+Boa) and started swinging. His fourth turn was less exciting as he sac'ed off a [Horizon Canopy](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horizon+Canopy) for a card and passed the turn.

Cheon dropped Korlash in the way. Well actually not exactly. [Mire Boa](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mire+Boa) was coming through anyway and Korlash was currently smaller than [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

Korlash stood in the way anyway and his Grandeur ability was used to find two extra swamps. Unfortunately it put a creature in the graveyard, growing [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) to 5/6. This meant Korlash just died.

I wasn't sure exactly what happened there, but it became apparent a turn later as Cheon cast [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings) to fetch [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption). The extra swamps meant the Tendrils was big enough to kill the two mana 5/6.

*EDIT: I asked Paul about this afterwards.*

![Whirling Dervish](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Whirling+Dervish)

*"No, I screwed up"-*Paul Cheon*.*

Imperiale made a second [Mire Boa](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mire+Boa) and grafted a couple of +1/+1 counters on it. Cheon was getting in control now though. Another [Careful Consideration](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Consideration) found [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation).

Imperiale followed with two [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

Cheon cast another [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation). Then he fetched Teferi with [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings).

It was now time for the end game.

However the green-white end game is pretty good. Imperiale dropped a [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer). The only thing that really takes that down is [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) and Cheon had already cast two of them.

Well not only [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation). Cheon shut it down with [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation).

Imperiale decided it was enough when Cheon tutored for another [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption).

Cheon 1  
 Imperiale 0

Imperiale led off with slow lands. [Terramorphic Expanse](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terramorphic+Expanse) brought a forest in tapped and [Vesuva](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vesuva) became a forest that came in tapped. Imperiale was then finally able to make some sideboard tech in [Whirling Dervish](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whirling+Dervish). The old mainstay from Legends is a terror on black decks.

This was before they decided to give black [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God). [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide) gave the Dervish wings but then ultimately just became a Griffin when [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) hit the board.

Imperiale followed up with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and then [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide)d the Griffin token.

Cheon bounced the token with a [Snapback](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapback). Ouch, that had to hurt.

![Triskelavus](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Triskelavus)

The US champ was looking dominant again. A second [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) cleared out the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and he followed it with [Shadowmage Infiltrator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shadowmage+Infiltrator).

Green-white does have an endgame and it is [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer). The pro-black Enforcer can only really be killed by [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) and Cheon had already used two of them.

So now with the [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) Imperiale would surely…

[Triskelavus](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Triskelavus)!

Ah, yeah, that sort of holds off Enforcer for a while.

But it's only going to hold off Enforcer for so long. Eventually it's going to run out of counters.

Teachings for [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink).

Ah yeah. I think someone was ready for this. With Cheon able to block the Enforcer for another six turns at least Imperiale packed it in.

Paul Cheon beats Jason Imperiale 2-0 and advances to the Semi-Final

  

## Sunday, June 24: 9:17 p.m. - Semifinals: Paul Cheon vs. Guillame Wafo-Tapa

*by Craig Jones*

 

Guillaume is playing a sliver deck blah, blah, blah. See the quarterfinals coverage for the low-down on his genius deck.

![Draining Whelk](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Draining+Whelk)

Paul is playing a B/U Korlash deck splashing for [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings) to provide a tool box including Pull From Eternity, Haunthing Hymm, [Disenchant](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disenchant), [Draining Whelk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Draining+Whelk), and [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption). The real innovation of this deck is his inclusion of [Psychotic Episode](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychotic+Episode) in the maindeck, which should prove quite disruptive against Guillaume's sliver engine. Paul's deck doesn't have any counters to deal with [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair), so he will have to draw numerous [Psychotic Episode](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychotic+Episode)s or time his Haunting Hymm perfectly if he wants to come out of this semifinal match victorious.

### Game 1

Both players developed their mana, which is a increasingly popular trend in this top 8, before making any plays. Cheon powered out a pair of [Prismatic Lens](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prismatic+Lens) before using [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption) to take care of Wafo-Tapa's [Gemhide Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemhide+Sliver). On Wafo's turn he played out some [Coalition Relic](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Coalition+Relic)'s, but still only had two lands to work with.

Cheon put a 3/3 Korlash into play on his turn, and Guillaume slipped a [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) into play courtesy of the explosive Relic's. All Paul could do was pass the turn after attacking Wafo-Tapa and playing his third Lens of the game.

Another [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) came down for Guillaume along with a third land. Paul played an [Urborg, Tomb of Yawgmoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Urborg%2C+Tomb+of+Yawgmoth) on his turn and cracked in for 5 before passing the turn to Wafo-Tapa, who just passed the turn back to Cheon after drawing.

Cheon attempted a [Psychotic Episode](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychotic+Episode) on his next turn and Guillaume used [Mystic Snake](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Snake) to counter it and bring Dormant and [Frenetic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frenetic+Sliver).

Cheon asked for the oracle text of [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) and a nameless judge scurried off to get it...

Three minutes later when he returned the board was clogged with slivers and Wafo had a million cards in his hand thanks to [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver) and [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion) tricks. Telekinetic tapped out Paul's blue sources, but he still managed to cast a [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation), which was answered with a [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion) into Venser.

Just when I thought things couldn't get any weirder a [Might Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Might+Sliver) poped out of Wafo's hand which gave his six slivers enough power to take Neon Cheon out with some hasty help from Reflex sliver.

Guillaume Wafo Tapa 1-0

### Game 2

At this point no one is even watching Kenji in the other semifinals match and all eyes are focused on Wafo-Tapa and his sliver gang.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d1/c8/d1c8c1e43ea2c20a428dbd6821398796f37e8814350de4e3bd7fafe87dc92e3e.jpg)*Double the pleasure, double the fun.*

Paul drove the action with a turn three [Shadowmage Infiltrator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shadowmage+Infiltrator), but Wafo responded with a [Reflex Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflex+Sliver) on his third turn with the help of a Gemhide. After Neon and Wafo-Tapa traded a couple of hits back and forth Cheon resolved a [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings) for [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption) at the end of Wafo's turn.

Cheon made no play on his turn and discarded a [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) during his cleanup. Wafo continued to beat until Paul cast an end of turn Tendrils but a [Mystic Snake](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Snake) appeared and sent the Tendrils packing. On Neon's next turn he dropped the bomb and made Wafo-Tapa discard his hand with a well placed Haunting Hymm.

Wafo-Tapa continued with the aggro approach when he bashed in again with his slivers and snake, bringing Paul to five. Another Tendrils took care of [Reflex Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflex+Sliver) and Wafo-Tapa was in serious trouble. Flashback on [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings) gave Cheon another Tendrils and the game was all but locked up when he took a Forsee off the top of WafoTapa's deck thanks to a [Psychotic Episode](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychotic+Episode).

Shadowmage was still providing Cheon with cards when Wafo-Tapa decked a [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver), which eventually proved useless due to Paul using [Draining Whelk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Draining+Whelk) on the next sliver he played.

Neon 1  
 Wafo-Tapa 1

### Game 3

Both players had streamlined draws including turn two Gemhide into turn three Relic for Tapa and turn three Shadowmage from Cheon. A Venser bounced Finkel back to Cheon's hand and on his next turn he used Tendrils to destroy Gemhide.

Frenetic came down for Tapa while Neon Cheon simply replayed his [Shadowmage Infiltrator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shadowmage+Infiltrator). [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver) came down giving him four cards in hand, which were discarded on Cheon's next turn with a timely Haunting Hymm. Cheon has found a way to dismantle Wafo's deck once again and Tapa's tight game was starting to unravel. At the end of Paul's turn in a desperate attempt to get some momentum he used his Frentic Slivers ability on itself, but unfortunately he lost his first die roll of the tournament and the sliver died.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b4/6c/b46c77dc2b3936eeb5084d711bee2b968f7c060c70aed5d58f3bf669e03bf5f7.jpg)*Paul Cheon had all the answers.*

On his next draw phase Wafo-Tapa drew a much needed [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee) which powered out a [Gemhide Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemhide+Sliver) and he was up and running again thanks to his [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver). Cheon in the meantime developed his board with another Finkel and continued to fill his hand with the one already in play.

A [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) caused a temporary headache for Cheon who was forced use a [Mystical Teachings](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Teachings) for [Disenchant](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disenchant) to get rid of it. Wafo's next turn saw him taping everything to suspend a [Detritivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detritivore) for three. Big daddy Korlash came down and Tendrils took care of [Dormant Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dormant+Sliver). Now Cheon was ready to kick it into full gear as he sent in the troops. Wafo blocked Korlash with Venser and then played a [Whitemane Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whitemane+Lion) to get it back, but Cheon had the [Slaughter Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Pact) and Venser was sent to the graveyard.

At the end of Wafo's turn Cheon used Mystical to get a [Draining Whelk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Draining+Whelk) and Tapa offered him his hand.

Neon Cheon takes down Wafo-Tapa 2-1!

  

## Sunday, June 24: 9:24 p.m. - Semifinals: Celso Zampere vs. Kenji Tsumura

*by Craig Jones*

 

![Sunlance](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Sunlance)

There are only two rounds left for theese last four players. In this Semi-Final Kenji Tsumura's mono-blue deck got to face off against Celso Zampere's green-white [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) deck.

Tsumura won the die roll and kicked off with some suspend cards, first an [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision) and then a [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate). Zampere tried to bust them up with successive [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper)s. The first took care of the Vision, but Kenji was in no mood to lose the Cloudskate and sent the [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) into ironic suspension with [Delay](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delay).

Zampere pulled some nice cycling tricks with [Flagstones of Trokair](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flagstones+of+Trokair) and [Edge of Autumn](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Edge+of+Autumn) but was starting to get that repeating yourself endlessly problem when playing against mono-blue. The Cloudskate came in and bounced something. A Shapeshifter came in as a Cloudskate and bounced something else. Then another Shapeshifter came in as a Cloudskate.

Boing, boing, boing.

Despite the yo-yo nature of his creatures Zampere got the [Delay](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delay)ed [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) back and started swinging with it, [Saffi Eriksdotter](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Saffi+Eriksdotter) and a [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger).

Zampere had already lost one Elephant token to Cloudskate bouncing, but tried again with a flashed back [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd). Despite all the tricks Kenji had only one card in hand left and was down to 9 life. He turned both of his Shapeshifters into [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger)s and got in for six damage to leave Zampere at 8. Oh and of course they copied the vigilance ability also so Tsumura still got to keep them on defence.

Zampere wasn't going to let a pair of copied Avengers scare him off. Both his Elephant and Avenger were big enough to take a Shapeshifter down and a [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide) put the [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) into the same category.

Tsumura played safe and put both Shapeshifters in the way of the Avenger in case of any tricks. The other creatures got in for enough to put Tsumura to two life.

In contrast to Tsumura's rather small hand of one, Zampere had four cards to play with. It's not often you seen green-white beating blue in the card advantage stakes. Zampere notched up the pressure with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and Tsumura scooped after failing to draw [Evacuation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Evacuation), Upheaval or any number of cards that aren't currently in this block.

Zampere 1  
 Tsumura 0

Zampere seemed to have a very slow start in Game 2. He didn't make a play until an Elephant arrived on the field on turn three. [Venser, Shaper Savant](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Venser%2C+Shaper+Savant) was waiting for it and [Llanowar Reborn](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Reborn) coming in tapped delayed flashing it back for a turn.

![Snapback](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Snapback)

Zampere wanted to get [Serrated Arrows](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serrated+Arrows) down first. The flashed back [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) was [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel)led and Zampere shot Venser with a [Sunlance](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunlance). What had he done with his green-white beatdown deck, turned it into a control deck?

Surely this was playing into Tsumura's hands.

[Delay](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delay) put a [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) into suspension for three turns and now Kenji had seven land and an [Urza's Factory](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Urza%27s+Factory) to start cranking out 2/2 tokens.

A [Cloudchaser Kestrel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudchaser+Kestrel) came in and then went back again thanks to [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate).

At least Zampere had more cards in hand.

Despite my joshing, Zampere actually wasn't looking in too bad a way. His [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) came back out of suspension and was joined by a second. There was no threshold yet, but it would only be a matter of time.

The Arrows shot down Tsumura's Cloudskate and allowed the [Cloudchaser Kestrel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudchaser+Kestrel) to level the life totals at 12 each.

Tsumura had three Factory Tokens, but the ground was clogged up anyway. Of more concern were the two Enforcers about to gain flying and dragon-sized bodies any time soon.

The Japanese star went for the first part of the pickles lock with [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental). He made a face down morph.

Zampere was on very dangerous ground as the morph flipped to reveal the Shapeshifter. The lock was in place but hadn't yet been clamped down fully. Zampere had mana to play with and just needed to get two cards in the graveyard to attack for a lethal 12 with his two Enforcers.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f8/6f/f86f9a452396a4fe96bd695d787acb16e87f53e0065d5819872f5069e8ec6fb9.jpg)*Traitor!*

The next turn Zampere found a second Flagstones. The legendary lands blowing up gave him threshold. He cast [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation) to prevent the Shapeshifter from blocking and went in for lethal damage.

But not quite…

[Snapback](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapback)! From the one card Tsumura had drawn that turn.

Zampere visibly slumped.

Now the lock clamped tight and Tsumura ended the game with a few quick swings of his factory tokens.

Zampere 1-1 Tsumura.

Zampere performed the cycle [Edge of Autumn](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Edge+of+Autumn) with Flagstones trick and then followed it up with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). It was the 'fair' version of [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) - just a 2/3 for two mana.

He followed with a [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) (with a +1/+1 token grafted on it) to clean up Tsumura's suspended Cloudskate and started hitting. Tsumura was able to [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel) a [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd), but was facing a very fast clock.

A [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) was [Cancel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cancel)led, but this again wasn't stopping the seven points of damage hitting him every turn. The Japanese player was down to a fragile seven life and in danger of slipping out of the tournament.

He made a morph and revealed it to be a Shapeshifter (copying [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)) when Zampere tried to [Sunlance](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunlance) it. It at least held the [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) off as Zampere crashed the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) through to drop Tsumura to three life.

Zampere dropped [Serrated Arrows](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serrated+Arrows) to make combat math even trickier for the Japanese player. Kenji was forced to make a [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental) just as a Grey Ogre to chump with or die.

Zampere shot it with Arrows at end of turn. He then thought for a while in his turn. Morphs are so good, because you're never exactly sure what they are.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9d/4f/9d4f9efeda8054d4983cd561bfe9f344c8d0e899bfd509ac6113b2d66df996a5.jpg)*Kenji Tsumara couldn't weather the Green/White beats*

He pointed the Arrows at the [Brine Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brine+Elemental) again to kill it and then entered the red zone with the 3/3 [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

And once against Zampere saw a lethal attack thwarted by [Snapback](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapback). It was even worse as [Desert](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Desert) meant Zampere's [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) died. He now had nothing.

This green-white deck is actually fairly resilient though. Zampere went from no creatures to two [Cloudchaser Kestrel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudchaser+Kestrel)s and two [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper)s in two turns. Tsumura's Shapeshifter wasn't holding that off even by masquerading as a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

Once again Kenji Tsumura was thwarted in the top 8.

Celso Zampere beats Kenji Tsumura 2-1 and advances to the final.

  

## Sunday, June 24: 9:35 p.m. - Finals: Celso Zampere vs. Paul Cheon

*by Noah Weil*

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/38/5f/385fea045ac0d63279f5241577f7b7241ccd15e4542b0d59adb1bce8555cf6bc.jpg)*Paul Cheon was ready to battle*

Things began in an unusual fashion, a missing Celso Zampere?

Head judge Jason Ness: "Where is Celso?"  
 Random Bystander: "He's watching Paulo and Willy draft. Or he might be in a side event"

Zampere finally came to his summons and warmly greeted his opponent. The players looked loose and relaxed as they shuffled for this final match, their eighteenth round. They spent discussed San Diego teamups and PT point levels. But when dice were rolled, luck was wished and mulligans were decided, the competitors got down to business.

### Game 1

Zampere did what his deck does best, starting with a graft land into a larger [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Cheon's first move was a [Psychotic Episode](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychotic+Episode), prompting a puzzled read from Zampere and a laugh from the audience.

"They always do that," said Cheon.

Goyf still came in for 3 and after combat, Zampere played an [Edge of Autumn](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Edge+of+Autumn) and another [Llanowar Reborn](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Reborn). [Careful Consideration](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Consideration) from Paul gave him options, but was certain to create an even larger 'Goyf. The [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) took on a [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide) while Cheon was tapped out, dealing even more. It took a [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) of course, but a Griffin was ready to take up the mantle. A brief rules skirmish occurred when Zampere started to untap and then tried to graft onto the token. The judge determined he had passed that option. A Call token did take one on, only to see both dice go to the grave via another [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation). Zampere restored with an unthreshed [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer), enhanced with yet another graft token. Cheon looked troubled and played the only card large enough to stand in the way, a 4/4 [Triskelavus](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Triskelavus). Zampere pondered and swung in with the Enforcer, to which Cheon let it through, bringing him down to five. A gigantic 5/6 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) came down after. Paul could only pass with [Triskelavus](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Triskelavus) and [Urza's Factory](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Urza%27s+Factory) mana available. [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation) prompted serious counting from Paul Cheon, to which he finally responded by a [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption) on the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Thrill saved it, until a Trike token spawned and shot it down.

Still unthreshed, [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) attacked, to be blocked by another trike and fired at Zampere; his first damage of the match. Cheon needed a [Vesuvan Shapeshifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vesuvan+Shapeshifter) to make a gigantic creature, so he dug with another Careful. Another Episode was madnessed out, revealing a [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) on top of the library. Paul well knew how awful it was to Psych Episode a top of the library card, and his mutterings drew gentle laughter from the crowd. With a sigh of resignation, [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) was condemned to the bottom.

Enforcer took an Isolation of its own, but [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide) off the top was a nice pull for Zampere. The final token blocked and shot Celso, who still had his 4/4 to play with.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d3/3a/d33a8c5bb5157cd97008cf3a2ca5ff1e2007e225e780c7144e4bf9c490a32c6c.jpg)*4/4 face off*

Fatty fat Korlash hit as a 9/9 for the American, unable to block the [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide)d creature but effective at racing. Zampere played the last card in his hand, a [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger), and hoped his opponent had nothing. In came Korlash, shaving Zampere's life total in half. Paul ended by cycling [Tolaria West](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tolaria+West) for a [Slaughter Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Pact). In came the 4/4 flier and 3/3 flier. Paul again entered the tank, knowing one had to die and that [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) and [Thrill of the Hunt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrill+of+the+Hunt) resided in the grave. Paul ended up Pacting the [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide)d creature, knowing he now had three blockers to contend with on the following turn. In came Korlash for a desperation attack, but the elephant jumped in the way. Both fliers came in next and Cheon had to scoop.

Cheon-0  
 Zampere-1

Paul took out his discard suite and Pull From Eternity for more immediate answers to his opponent's blistering deck. Zampere exchanged some [Riftsweeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftsweeper) and [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger) for his controlling opponent.

### Game 2

Paul couldn't have been happy to start with a mulligan down a game, but Celso was had a considerate show of solidarity. Both kept with their next six and Paul led with the ever-popular [Terramorphic Expanse](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terramorphic+Expanse) against Zampere's graft land.

Celso's next move was a [Whirling Dervish](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Whirling+Dervish) who took on a speedier than normal +1/+1 counter. Dervish had been a winner all weekend for the Brazilian, whose turn two appearance had been giving control decks fits. Cheon merely got things organized with a transmuted for Urborg. Lens helped too, but Dervish was unrelenting. Zampere had no interesting in playing anything else; Dervish seemed strong enough on its own. Yet at the 4/4 stage a [Temporal Isolation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Temporal+Isolation) hit, prompting Celso to get on with the rest of his hand. [Mystic Enforcer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystic+Enforcer) continued the pro-black theme as Cheon looked stymied. He looked even more unhappy to the [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide) enchanting the Nomad Mystic. [Damnation](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Damnation) finally made an appearance, leaving behind a mere 2/2 fighter. Saffi and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) joined his action.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/8b/81/8b819e33b82a2dfdc4e79ffc161116a4a38a927ab43db1dbd2e8dbdd04a772e0.jpg)*Celso Zampere had too many threats.*

Paul pondered further, his back against the wall, and decided on a [Shadowmage Infiltrator](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shadowmage+Infiltrator) to help find help. An attempt at [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide), probably excessive, resulted in a [Slaughter Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Pact) on the 'Goyf. Saffi saved it, but at the cost of two of his three attackers. [Careful Consideration](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Consideration) dug further for the US Champ, but Finkel was needed on chump duty. Celso once again passed with his hand full of goodness, confident that Paul needed heroic efforts to escape. 6/7 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s are tricky, although 5/5 Korlash was a fine defender. It got even better when Granduer made it 7/7, the better to block the 'Goyf. Celso was hoping to tap some mana from Cheon, but ended up trading his creature for absolutely nothing. Unperturbed, another Dervish came to take up the fight. Of course the Griffin token had been pecking away the whole time. In the two came again, to which Cheon searched out a Venser. Venser bounced the token and made a beautiful blocker against the Dervish, an utterly sick turn for Cheon. And still Zampere wasn't out of gas; his opponent was just 2 life from giving Celso Zampere the title. That 2 life turned into 10 off a Tendrils, and now Korlash and Venser attacked unimpeded. Zampere literally slumped in his seat at the reversal, inches away from a GP victory but watching it slip through his fingers. [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink) was flashed on Venser to kill an elephant token, and another Tendrils took down the last blocker. Paul attacked for the kill. Zampere shook his head.

Cheon-1  
 Zampere-1

### Game 3

With a sincere exchange of good luck, the final game got underway. Both kept and began with mana fixing, Edges of Autumn and Terramoprhics Expanse. As to be expected, Celso had the first move with a [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd), and the flashback. Cheon went Teachings for an answer to the pair of 3/3s, ultimately deciding on [Tendrils of Corruption](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Corruption) (good answer). Paul had a tough call to make; Tendrils now to avoid Thrill, or Tendrils later to avoid [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide). He decided on an Urborg-powered, main-phase Corrupt, trying to keep himself afloat while his card advantage spells kicked in. Celso was having none of that, and replaced his fallen pachyderm with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), about to get bigger from Cheon's [Careful Consideration](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Consideration).

Against the tapped out opponent Celso went for [Griffin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griffin+Guide), which landed on an elephant. Nine points is a tough pill to swallow, even for national champions. Putting it on the token was interesting, as it saved 'Goyf from [Slaughter Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Pact) but made it far worse against Venser. And frankly, it was already pretty bad against Venser. In they came again, to which Cheon flashed back teachings to find an alt cast [Snapback](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapback). Two cards gone, although [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) took on a bonus from an enchantment hitting the yard. [Tolaria West](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tolaria+West) went for the popular [Slaughter Pact](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Pact), as Celso had given him another juicy target with [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger). Down came [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher), to bring back some of the life lost and combo up with Cheon's [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink)s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/be/ba/bebad658c96de6810cf3466f74fe3a5f99be6f0848c87ea9c3d6f741e408f548.jpg)*Grand Prix finalist Paul Cheon*

And that's just what happened as the Riftwatcher blocked an attacking Goyf and blinked for a reset and 4 more life, and a flashback to do it again. Celso was sick of an opponent gaining life on his attacks and slapped down a second [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger). Paul was sick of not having Korlash in play and fixed that situation too. It wasn't enough as one unblocked [Serra Avenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20211223151633/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serra+Avenger) got Thrilled and re-thrilled, dealing exactly five and giving Celso Zampere Jr. the game and the match.

Cheon-1  
 Zampere-2

Celso Zampere Jr. wins Grand Prix Montreal!
